  label: 5. Taddhitakaṇḍa
---

import { LinkCard } from "@astrojs/starlight/components";

:::note
The `taddhita` suffixes are grouped by the sense they express: descent (`apacca`), relation and the many other senses of `ṇika` and its kin, the abstract, comparison, possession, number and the adverbial suffixes. Each derivation shows the vuddhi, the elision of the marker `ṇ` and the sandhi that the Kaccāyana rules prescribe.

:::

## Apaccataddhita (Suffixes of descent)

> Atha nāmato eva vibhatyantā apaccādiatthavisese taddhituppattīti nāmato paraṃ taddhitavidhānamārabhīyate.

Now, because a `taddhita` (secondary derivative) arises only from a noun — from a stem that carries an inflection — in a particular sense such as "offspring", the treatment of the `taddhita` is begun after that of the noun [and its compounds].

> Tattha tasmā tividhaliṅgato paraṃ hutvā hitā sahitāti taddhitā, ṇā dipaccayānametaṃ adhivacanaṃ, tesaṃ vā nāmikānaṃ hitā upakārā taddhitāti anvatthabhūtaparasamaññāvasenāpi ṇā dippaccayāva taddhitā nāma.

Here `taddhita` means "placed (`hita`), that is, joined on, after (`paraṃ hutvā`) that (`tasmā`) stem of three genders"; it is a name for the suffixes `ṇa` and the rest. Or else they are `taddhita` because they are of service (`hitā`), helpful, to those (`tesaṃ`) nominal stems. So even by way of the terminology of the other grammars, which is admitted by [§9 (11): Parasamaññā payoge](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m9) and here says what it means (`anvattha`), it is precisely the suffixes `ṇa` and the rest that are called `taddhita`.

> ‘‘Vasiṭṭhassa apacca’’nti viggahe –

In the analysis (`viggaha`, the phrase the derivative stands for) "Vasiṭṭha's offspring" —

> ‘‘Liṅgañca nipaccate’’ti ito liṅga ggahaṇamanuvattate.

the word `liṅga` (stem) continues [into the rule] from [61 (§53): Liṅgañca nipaccate](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r61), Kaccāyana's [§53 (61): Liṅgañca nippajjate](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m53).

---

<a name="r361"></a>

### 361 (§344): Vā ṇa’pacce (Optionally `ṇa` in the sense of offspring) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§344 (361): Vāṇa’pacce" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m344" description="Optionally `ṇa` in the sense of offspring" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Buddhappiya opens the Taddhitakaṇḍa, his fifth section, with the same sutta that opens Kaccāyana's Taddhitakappa, and his own preface (above) has already said why the section comes here — a `taddhita` is built on an inflected noun, so it follows the noun, the `kāraka` and the compound — and what the word `taddhita` means. The section is the first of his seven kinds of derivative, the `Apaccataddhita` or suffixes of descent, 361–372; but he does not go straight on to the second suffix of descent as Kaccāyana does. Having stated the rule and begun the analysis `vasiṭṭhassa apaccaṃ`, he interrupts the derivation of `vāsiṭṭho` four times to bring in the rules it needs — [362 (§432): Dhātuliṅgehi parā paccayā](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r362) on where the suffix stands, [363 (§396): Tesaṃ ṇo lopaṃ](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r363) on the marker `ṇ`, [364 (§400): Vuddhādisarassa vāsaṃyogantassa saṇe ca](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r364) and [365 (§405): Ayuvaṇṇānañcāyo vuddhī](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r365) on the strengthening of the first vowel — and only under 365 does the word reach its finished form. Kaccāyana had left all four of these where his topics put them: §396–§405 at the end of the chapter, §432 in the Ākhyātakappa.

His gloss adds two things to the Kaccāyana vutti. The base is named: `chaṭṭhiyantato liṅgamhā`, "after a stem ending in the sixth form ⑥" — the `tassa` of `tassa apaccaṃ` ("his offspring") is in ⑥, and so is `vasiṭṭhassa` in the analysis; Kaccāyana's vutti has only `tassāpacca` and leaves the form of the base to be inferred. And the `vā` is glossed at once: `vikappena`, "by option". In Ruiz-Falqués' terms (*Boundaries and Domains*, [§3.1](/reference/boundaries-and-domains---understanding-optionality-in-buddhappiyas-rūpasiddhi)) this is the plain `vikappa`, the open option, whose two branches are both good Pāli. But the two branches are not two derived forms. Buddhappiya says under 365 what the option is between: `tena sabbattha vākyavuttiyo bhavanti`, "hence everywhere both the phrase (`vākya`) and the derivative (`vutti`) occur" — `vāsiṭṭho` or `vasiṭṭhassa putto`, as his examples there put it. He also says there how far the `vā` reaches: `taddhitavidhāne sabbattha vattate`, "it continues everywhere in the treatment of the `taddhita`" — the `vā` is an `adhikāra` over the whole section, which is why his vutti can repeat `hoti vā` in [366 (§345): Ṇāyana ṇāna vacchādito](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r366) and the rules after it, and why a rule whose Kaccāyana vutti has no `vā` at all, [378 (§353): Jātādīnamimiyā ca](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r378), still receives one from him. Twice he puts the carried `vā` to a second use, `puna vāggahaṇena`, to admit a further suffix or a further sense — under [368 (§347): Ato ṇi vā](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r368) and [373 (§350): Yena vā saṃsaṭṭhaṃ tarati carati vahati ṇiko](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r373), the two suttas in which Kaccāyana writes `vā` again although it is already in force.

:::

> Chaṭṭhiyantato liṅgamhā ṇa ppaccayo hoti vikappena ‘‘tassa apacca’’miccetasmiṃ atthe.

After a stem ending in the sixth form ⑥, the suffix `ṇa` is added by option (`vikappena`) in the sense "his offspring" (`tassa apaccaṃ`).

> So ca –

And that [suffix] —

:::tip[Example]

The analysis `vasiṭṭhassa apaccaṃ` ("Vasiṭṭha's offspring") is the phrase from which the derivative is built; the suffix is added to `vasiṭṭhassa`, the stem `vasiṭṭha` with the inflection `sa` of ⑥⨀.

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(vasiṭṭha + ṇa) | vasiṭṭhassa apaccaṃ → vāsiṭṭho | a descendant of Vasiṭṭha — or the phrase `vasiṭṭhassa apaccaṃ` |

|     | vasiṭṭha + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇa        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vasiṭṭhassa + ṇa                | §344 |

The rules that follow take the derivation on, one step each, to `vāsiṭṭho` under [365 (§405): Ayuvaṇṇānañcāyo vuddhī](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r365).

:::

---

<a name="r362"></a>

### 362 (§432): Dhātuliṅgehi parā paccayā (suffixes come after roots and stems: a governing statement) :badge[sīhagatika]{variant=caution}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§432 (362): Dhātuliṅgehi parā paccayā" href="/kaccayana/6-akhyatakappa#m432" description="suffixes come after roots and stems: a governing statement" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

In Kaccāyana this sutta stands at the head of the verbal suffixes in the Ākhyātakappa (§432), and its vutti reaches back to `vāsiṭṭho` as its one nominal example. Buddhappiya moves it to the first place in his grammar where a suffix is added to a stem: `so ca`, "and that suffix", at the end of 361, is completed by this rule — it stands *after* the stem. He calls it a `paribhāsā`, an interpretive rule that governs the reading of other rules rather than prescribing an operation of its own, which is what the Kaccāyana page's `sīhagatika` badge marks: its glance falls back over the `taddhita` suffixes and forward over every verbal and `kita` suffix to come. His gloss adds three things: a double etymology of `paccaya`, "suffix" — that from which, depending on it (`paṭicca`), the meaning comes, or that by which meanings are understood (`patīyanti`); the maxim `vuttatthānamappayogo`, "what has been expressed is not used [again]", a `paribhāsā` of the tradition (not a Kaccāyana sutta) that he also uses in the Samāsakaṇḍa, and which here removes the word `apacca` from the analysis once `ṇa` has taken over its sense; and the elision of the inflection of the base by [332 (§317): Tesaṃ vibhattiyo lopā ca](/rupasiddhi/4-samasakanda#r332), whose `tesaṃ` he had already stretched, in that section, to cover derivatives as well as compounds. There is no option marker.

:::

> Dhātūhi, liṅgehi ca paccayā parāva hontīti paribhāsato apaccatthasambandhiliṅgato chaṭṭhiyantāyeva paro hoti. Paṭicca etasmā attho etīti paccayo, patīyanti anena atthāti vā paccayo, ‘‘vuttatthānamappayogo’’ti apacca saddassa appayogo, ‘‘tesaṃ vibhattiyo lopā ce’’ti vibhattilopo ca.

By the interpretive rule (`paribhāsā`) "the suffixes come only after roots (`dhātu`) and after stems (`liṅga`)", [the suffix `ṇa`] comes after the stem ending in the sixth form ⑥ which is connected with the sense of offspring. It is called a `paccaya` (suffix) because from it, depending on it (`paṭicca`), the meaning comes (`eti`); or because meanings are understood (`patīyanti`) by means of it. By [the maxim] "what has been expressed is not used [again]" (`vuttatthānaṃ appayogo`), the word `apacca` is not used [beside the derivative, since `ṇa` now expresses its sense]; and by [§317 (332): Tesaṃ vibhattiyo lopā ca](/kaccayana/4-samasakappa#m317) the inflection [of the base] is elided.

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(vasiṭṭha + ṇa) | vasiṭṭha + ṇa | the stem with its suffix, the inflection `sa` and the word `apacca` removed |

|     | vasiṭṭha + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇa        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vasiṭṭha + ~~sa~~ + ṇa          | §317 |

:::

---

<a name="r363"></a>

### 363 (§396): Tesaṃ ṇo lopaṃ (the `ṇ` of these suffixes is elided) :badge[utsarga]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§396 (363): Tesaṃ ṇo lopaṃ" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m396" description="the `ṇ` of these suffixes is elided" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana states this rule near the end of his chapter, among the operations common to all the suffixes (§396–§405), after every `ṇ` suffix has been taught. Buddhappiya brings it to the first derivation, the moment `ṇa` has been added to `vasiṭṭha`, because nothing further can be done until the marker is out of the way. His gloss names what the `ṇ` is: the suffixes are `ṇānubandhā`, "carrying `ṇ` as an `anubandha`" — a marker letter attached to the name of a suffix to signal an operation, not a sound of the word. The operation it signals is the strengthening of the first vowel, stated in the next rule; that signalled, the `ṇ` goes, and `ṇa` adds only `a`. The rule has no condition and no option marker, and every `ṇ` derivation in the section cites it; the Ākhyāta section has the same device for the causative suffixes ([526 (§523): Kāritānaṃ ṇo lopaṃ](/rupasiddhi/6-akhyatakanda#r526)).

:::

> Tesaṃ taddhitappaccayānaṃ ṇā nubandhānaṃ ṇa kāro lopamāpajjate.

Of these `taddhita` suffixes that carry the marker `ṇ` (`ṇānubandha`), the letter `ṇ` is elided.

:::tip[Example]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(vasiṭṭha + ṇa) | vasiṭṭha + a | the suffix reduced to `a` |

|     | vasiṭṭha + ~~sa~~ + ṇa          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vasiṭṭha + (~~ṇ~~a)             | §396 |

:::

---

<a name="r364"></a>

### 364 (§400): Vuddhādisarassa vāsaṃyogantassa saṇe ca (optionally `vuddhi` of the first vowel not before a conjunct, before a `ṇ` suffix) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§400 (364): Vuddhādisarassa vā’saṃyogantassa saṇe ca" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m400" description="optionally `vuddhi` of the first vowel not before a conjunct, before a `ṇ` suffix" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

This is the operation the marker `ṇ` announces, and Buddhappiya brings it forward from Kaccāyana's §400, five suttas from the end of the chapter, to the point where the first `ṇ` derivation needs it; the definition of `vuddhi` itself, Kaccāyana's very last sutta, follows as [365 (§405): Ayuvaṇṇānañcāyo vuddhī](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r365). His gloss reads the sutta twice. On the first reading the `vā` is "or", as in Kaccāyana's vutti: the strengthening falls on the initial vowel (`alasa`, `ālasyaṃ`) *or* on the vowel of the initial consonant (`vasiṭṭha`, `vāsiṭṭho`) — `ādisarassa vā ādibyañjanassa vā` — and he defines `saṃyoganta`, "ending in a conjunct", as a vowel followed by a double consonant, so that `asaṃyoganta` excludes `bhaggu`, `vaccha`, `kattikā` and the like from the rule. On the second reading (`atha vā`) the `vā` is an option marker, and a restricted one: `vavatthitavibhāsatthoyaṃ vāsaddo`, the standard formula that Ruiz-Falqués examines (*Boundaries and Domains*, [§3.2–§3.3](/reference/boundaries-and-domains---understanding-optionality-in-buddhappiyas-rūpasiddhi)). A `vavatthitavibhāsā` is not a free choice between two forms but an option whose outcome is fixed word by word, and his verse states its three domains: the strengthening is `nicca`, invariable, in `vāsiṭṭha` and its kind (only `vāsiṭṭho`, never `vasiṭṭho`); `anicca`, variable, in `oḷumpika` and its kind (`oḷumpiko` and `uḷumpiko` both occur, as he shows under [373 (§350): Yena vā saṃsaṭṭhaṃ tarati carati vahati ṇiko](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r373)); and `asanta`, absent, in the colour words `nīla`, `pīta` and the like, where the dyed cloth is `nīlaṃ`, not `nelaṃ` — the exception Kaccāyana had to state in a verse of his own under §352. The `ca`, which Kaccāyana's vutti used to bring in "the vowel of the initial consonant", Buddhappiya takes as `avadhāraṇa`, delimitation: strengthening happens before a `ṇ` suffix and there only. He then announces the next rule as a `paribhāsā` that fixes what `vuddhi` means, since the term would otherwise be indeterminate.

:::

> Ādisarassa vā ādibyañjanassa vā asaṃyogantassa vuddhi hoti saṇa kārappaccaye pare. Saṃyogo anto assāti saṃyoganto, tadañño asaṃyoganto. Atha vā vavatthitavibhāsatthoyaṃ vā saddo, tadā ca pakatibhūte liṅge sarānamādisarassa asaṃyogantassa vuddhi hoti saṇa kāre taddhitappaccaye pareti attho.

Of the initial vowel, or of [the vowel of] the initial consonant, when it is not followed by a conjunct, there is `vuddhi` (strengthening) when a suffix with the letter `ṇ` follows. That which has a conjunct (`saṃyoga`) after it is `saṃyoganta`, "ending in a conjunct"; what is other than that is `asaṃyoganta`. Or else this word `vā` has the sense of a restricted option (`vavatthitavibhāsā`, an option whose outcome is fixed case by case); and then the meaning is: among the vowels of the stem that is the base, the initial vowel not followed by a conjunct takes `vuddhi` when a `taddhita` suffix with `ṇ` follows.

> Tena –

Hence —

> Vāsiṭṭhādīsu niccāyaṃ, aniccoḷumpikādisu; \
> Na vuddhi nīlapītādo, vavatthitavibhāsato.

In `vāsiṭṭha` and the like this [strengthening] is invariable (`nicca`); in `oḷumpika` and the like it is not invariable (`anicca`); \
in `nīla`, `pīta` and the like there is no strengthening — by the restricted option.

> Ca saddaggahaṇamavadhāraṇatthaṃ.

The word `ca` is taken in the sense of delimitation (`avadhāraṇa`: before a `ṇ` suffix, and only there).

> Tassā vuddhiyā aniyamappasaṅge niyamatthaṃ paribhāsamāha.

Since [without a definition] that `vuddhi` would be left indeterminate, he states an interpretive rule (`paribhāsā`) to fix it.

:::tip[Examples]

The three domains of the restricted option, in the words the verse names:

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(vasiṭṭha + ṇa) | vāsiṭṭho | a descendant of Vasiṭṭha — `nicca`: the strengthening is invariable |
| 🚹①⨀(uḷumpa + ṇika) | oḷumpiko, uḷumpiko | one who crosses by raft — `anicca`: with or without the strengthening |
| 🚻①⨀(nīla + ṇa) | nīlaṃ | dyed blue — `asanta`: no strengthening in the colour words |
| 🚻①⨀(pīta + ṇa) | pītaṃ | dyed yellow |

|     | vasiṭṭha + (~~ṇ~~a)             | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | v + (a→ā) + siṭṭha + a          | §400 |

The strengthened vowel is `ā` by the next rule, which defines `vuddhi`; `bhaggavo` (`bhaggu` + `ṇava`) and `kaccāyano` (`kacca` + `ṇāyana`) keep their vowel, because a conjunct follows it (`asaṃyogantassa`).

:::

---

<a name="r365"></a>

### 365 (§405): Ayuvaṇṇānañcāyo vuddhī (the `vuddhi` of `a`, `i`, `u` is `ā`, `e`, `o`) :badge[anvattha]{variant=note}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§405 (365): Ayuvaṇṇānañcāyo vuddhi" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m405" description="the `vuddhi` of `a`, `i`, `u` is `ā`, `e`, `o`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana closes his Taddhitakappa with this definition; Buddhappiya makes it the fifth sutta of his Taddhitakaṇḍa, the `paribhāsā` he promised at the end of [364 (§400): Vuddhādisarassa vāsaṃyogantassa saṇe ca](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r364), because the first derivation cannot proceed until `vuddhi` has a value. He takes the sutta apart word by word — `yu` is `i` and `u`, `yuvaṇṇa` the `i`- and `u`-sounds, `ayuvaṇṇa` `a` together with them, `āyo` the three vowels `ā`, `e`, `o` — and gives two readings of its `ca`: it includes (`sampiṇḍana`) the case of no strengthening, which is how the Bālāvatāra (¶232) takes it, or it delimits, "these and no others". The second `vuddhi` of the sutta (`puna vuddhiggahaṇaṃ`) he explains as Kaccāyana's vutti does, as licensing the strengthening of a later member of a compound (`negamajānapadā`); and the naming of the domain, `ayuvaṇṇānaṃ`, as blocking any strengthening of `ā`, `e`, `o` themselves — his verse: a vowel already strengthened is not strengthened again, and `ā`, `e`, `o` are strengthened by nature. That is why `gotamo` and `bhāradvājo` keep their vowels, below.

He then finishes the derivation begun at 361, quoting each rule that fires: elision of the final `a` of the base before the `a` of the suffix by [67 (§83): Saralopo’mādesappaccayādimhi saralope tu pakati](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r67), carrying the consonant over to the following vowel by [14 (§11): Naye paraṃ yutte](/rupasiddhi/1-sandhikanda#r14), the designation of the derivative as a noun by [334 (§601): Taddhitasamāsakitakā nāmaṃvā’tavetunādīsu ca](/rupasiddhi/4-samasakanda#r334) and the addition of the inflections. Three remarks are his own. The name passes to the grandson and great-grandson by extension (`upacāra`), and in a clan derivative the suffix is always added to the first base — to `vasiṭṭha`, never to `vāsiṭṭha`. The feminine `vāsiṭṭhī` takes `ī` by [190 (§239): Ṇava ṇika ṇeyya ṇantuhi](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r190) because the word ends in a `ṇa` suffix, and is a noun by the `ca` of 334, which extends that rule to words ending in a feminine suffix. And the derivative has all three genders (`tiliṅgatā`), agreeing with the person or with `apacca` — a point he asks the reader to carry through the whole section. His examples differ from Kaccāyana's in one respect: Kaccāyana derives `bhāradvājo` and `vesāmitto` from `bharadvāja` and `visāmitta` with strengthening, Buddhappiya from `bhāradvāja` and `vesāmitta`, so that for him these names, like `gotamo`, show no strengthening at all. The last block states the reach of the `vā` of [361 (§344): Vā ṇa’pacce](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r361) — everywhere in the section, giving phrase and derivative side by side — and of `apacce`, which continues down to [373 (§350): Yena vā saṃsaṭṭhaṃ tarati carati vahati ṇiko](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r373), where `saṃsaṭṭha` replaces it.

:::

> Tesaṃ a kārai vaṇṇu vaṇṇānameva yathākkamaṃ ā eo iccete vuddhiyo honti. Ca saddaggahaṇamavuddhisampiṇḍanatthaṃ, avadhāraṇatthaṃ vā. I ca u ca yu, yu eva vaṇṇā yuvaṇṇā, a ca yuvaṇṇā ca ayuvaṇṇā. Ā ca e ca o ca āyo. Puna vuddhi ggahaṇaṃ ‘‘negamajānapadā’’tiādīsu uttarapadavuddhibhāvatthaṃ, ettha ca ‘‘ayuvaṇṇāna’’nti ṭhānaniyamavacanaṃ āyonaṃ vuddhibhāvappasaṅganivattanatthaṃ.

Of those — the letter `a`, the `i`-sound and the `u`-sound — and of those alone, `ā`, `e` and `o` respectively are the `vuddhi`. The word `ca` is taken either to include (`sampiṇḍana`) the absence of strengthening [in some words], or for delimitation. `i` and `u` are `yu`; the sounds that are just `yu` are `yuvaṇṇā`; `a` together with the `yuvaṇṇā` are `ayuvaṇṇā`. `ā` and `e` and `o` are `āyo`. The second mention of `vuddhi` is so that there may be strengthening of the later member [of a compound] in `negamajānapadā` ("born in towns and provinces") and the like; and here the statement `ayuvaṇṇānaṃ`, which fixes the locus, is to ward off any thought that `ā`, `e`, `o` might themselves be strengthened.

> Yathā hi katavuddhīnaṃ, puna vuddhi na hotiha; \
> Tathā sabhāvavuddhīnaṃ, āyonaṃ puna vuddhi na.

For just as vowels whose strengthening has been made receive no second strengthening here, \
so `ā`, `e` and `o`, strengthened by nature, receive no further strengthening.

> Tato a kārassa ā kāro vuddhi, ‘‘saralopomādesappaccayādimhi saralope tu pakatī’’ti saralopapakatibhāvā, ‘‘naye paraṃ yutte’’ti paranayanaṃ katvā taddhitattā ‘‘taddhitasamāsa’’iccādinā nāmabyapadese kate pure viya syā dyuppatti.

Then the `a` [of `vasiṭṭha`] takes `ā` as its strengthening; by [§83 (67): Saralopo’ mādesa paccayādimhi saralope tu pakati](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m83) the [final] vowel [of the base] is elided and [the vowel of the suffix] keeps its original form; the [consonant] is carried over to the following [vowel] by [§11 (14): Naye paraṃ yutte](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m11); and since it is a `taddhita`, once it has been designated a noun by [§601 (334): Taddhitasamāsakitakā nāmaṃ vā’ tave tunādīsu ca](/kaccayana/7-kibbidhanakappa#m601), the inflections `si` and the rest are added as before.

> Vāsiṭṭho, vasiṭṭhassa putto vā, vāsiṭṭhā iccādi purisa saddasamaṃ. Tassa nattupanattādayopi tadupacārato vāsiṭṭhāyeva. Evaṃ sabbattha gottataddhite paṭhamappakatitoyeva paccayo hoti.

`vāsiṭṭho`, or [the phrase] `vasiṭṭhassa putto` ("Vasiṭṭha's son"); `vāsiṭṭhā` and so on, like the word `purisa`. His grandsons, great-grandsons and the rest are also `vāsiṭṭhā`, by extension (`upacāra`) of that name. Thus everywhere, in a clan derivative (`gottataddhita`), the suffix is added only to the first base [the ancestor's name].

> Itthiyaṃ ṇappaccayantattā ‘‘ṇava ṇika ṇeyya ṇa ntūhī’’ti vāsiṭṭha saddato īpaccayo. Saralopādiṃ katvā itthippaccayantattā ‘‘taddhitasamāsa’’iccādisutte ca ggahaṇena nāmabyapadese kate syā dyuppatti, vāsiṭṭhī kaññā, vāsiṭṭhī, vāsiṭṭhiyo iccādi itthi saddasamaṃ.

In the feminine, because [the word] ends in the suffix `ṇa`, the suffix `ī` is added to the word `vāsiṭṭha` by [§239 (190): Ṇava ṇika ṇeyya ṇa ntuhi](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m239). After vowel elision and the rest, since [the word] now ends in a feminine suffix, it is designated a noun by the word `ca` in the sutta [§601] `Taddhitasamāsa…`, and the inflections `si` and the rest are added: `vāsiṭṭhī kaññā` ("a girl of Vasiṭṭha's line"); `vāsiṭṭhī`, `vāsiṭṭhiyo` and so on, like the word `itthī`.

> Napuṃsake vāsiṭṭhaṃ apaccaṃ, vāsiṭṭhāni apaccāni iccādi citta saddasamaṃ. Evaṃ uparipi taddhitantassa tiliṅgatā veditabbā.

In the neuter, `vāsiṭṭhaṃ apaccaṃ` ("Vasiṭṭha's offspring"), `vāsiṭṭhāni apaccāni` and so on, like the word `citta`. In the same way, further on too, a word ending in a `taddhita` suffix is to be understood as having all three genders.

> Bhāradvājassa putto bhāradvājo, vesāmittassa putto vesāmitto, gotamassa putto gotamo. Ettha ca ayuvaṇṇattābhāvā ā kārādīnaṃ na vuddhi hoti. Vasudevassa apaccaṃ vāsudevo, bāladevo[^1]. ‘‘Cittako’’tiādīsu pana saṃyogantattā vuddhi na bhavati.

Bhāradvāja's son is `bhāradvājo`, Vesāmitta's son `vesāmitto`, Gotama's son `gotamo`; here `ā` and the other [first vowels] are not strengthened, because they are not `a`, `i` or `u`. Vasudeva's offspring is `vāsudevo`; [Baladeva's] `bāladevo`. In `cittako` and the like, on the other hand, there is no strengthening because [the first vowel] is followed by a conjunct.

> ‘‘Vacchassa apacca’’nti viggahe ṇa mhi sampatte ‘‘vā ṇa’pacce’’ti ito vā ti taddhitavidhāne sabbattha vattate, tena sabbattha vākyavuttiyo bhavanti. ‘‘Apacce’’ti padaṃ yāva saṃsaṭṭha ggahaṇāva vattate.

In the analysis "Vaccha's offspring", when `ṇa` would apply [by 361, the next rule intervenes]. The `vā` from [361 (§344): Vā ṇa’pacce](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r361) continues everywhere in the treatment of the `taddhita`; hence everywhere both the phrase (`vākya`) and the derivative (`vutti`) occur. The word `apacce` continues as far as the mention of `saṃsaṭṭha` [in 373].

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(vasiṭṭha + ṇa) | vāsiṭṭho | a descendant of Vasiṭṭha — or `vasiṭṭhassa putto` |
| 🚹①⨂(vasiṭṭha + ṇa) | vāsiṭṭhā | descendants of Vasiṭṭha; also his grandsons and great-grandsons, by extension |
| 🚺①⨀(vasiṭṭha + ṇa + ī) | vāsiṭṭhī kaññā | a girl of Vasiṭṭha's line |
| 🚺①⨂(vasiṭṭha + ṇa + ī) | vāsiṭṭhī, vāsiṭṭhiyo | women of Vasiṭṭha's line |
| 🚻①⨀(vasiṭṭha + ṇa) | vāsiṭṭhaṃ apaccaṃ | Vasiṭṭha's offspring, agreeing with `apacca` |
| 🚻①⨂(vasiṭṭha + ṇa) | vāsiṭṭhāni apaccāni | Vasiṭṭha's offspring (plural) |
| 🚹①⨀(bhāradvāja + ṇa) | bhāradvājo | Bhāradvāja's son — no strengthening, the first vowel being already `ā` |
| 🚹①⨀(vesāmitta + ṇa) | vesāmitto | Vesāmitta's son — the first vowel already `e` |
| 🚹①⨀(gotama + ṇa) | gotamo | Gotama's son — the first vowel already `o` |
| 🚹①⨀(vasudeva + ṇa) | vāsudevo | Vasudeva's offspring — `a` to `ā` |
| 🚹①⨀(baladeva + ṇa) | bāladevo | Baladeva's offspring — `a` to `ā` |
| 🚹①⨀(cittaka + ṇa) | cittako | Cittaka's offspring — no strengthening before the conjunct `tt` |

The derivation begun at 361, complete:

|     | vasiṭṭha + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇa        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vasiṭṭha + ~~sa~~ + ṇa          | §317 |
| →   | vasiṭṭha + (~~ṇ~~a)             | §396 |
| →   | v + (a→ā) + siṭṭha + a          | §400, §405 |
| →   | vāsiṭṭh + (~~a~~ + a)           | §83  |
| →   | vāsiṭṭha                        | §11  |
| →   | vāsiṭṭha + si (a noun)          | §601 |
| →   | vāsiṭṭha + (si→o)               | §104 |
| →   | **vāsiṭṭho**                    |      |

The first form ⨀ `o` is that of [§104 (66): So](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m104); the plural `vāsiṭṭhā` and the rest follow the paradigm of `purisa`. The feminine and the neuter:

|     | vāsiṭṭha (🚺) + ī + si          | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vāsiṭṭh + (~~a~~ + ī) + si      | §239, §83 |
| →   | vāsiṭṭhī + si (a noun)          | §601 |
| →   | vāsiṭṭhī + ~~si~~               | §220 |
| →   | **vāsiṭṭhī**                    |      |

|     | vāsiṭṭha (🚻) + si              | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vāsiṭṭha + (si→aṃ)              | §219 |
| →   | **vāsiṭṭhaṃ**                   |      |

The feminine `si` is elided by [§220 (74): Sesato lopaṃ gasipi](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m220), the neuter `si` becomes `aṃ` by [§219 (195): Siṃ](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m219); `vāsiṭṭhiyo` and `vāsiṭṭhāni` follow the paradigms of `itthī` and `citta`.

:::

[^1]: CST4 prints `baladevo`; the word stands among the examples of strengthening, and Kaccāyana's vutti under §344 and Buddhappiya's own `bāladevi` under 368 have the `ā`.

---

<a name="r366"></a>

### 366 (§345): Ṇāyana ṇāna vacchādito (`ṇāyana`, `ṇāna` after `vaccha` and the like) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§345 (366): Ṇāyana ṇāna vacchādito" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m345" description="`ṇāyana`, `ṇāna` after `vaccha` and the like" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

With the apparatus of 362–365 in hand, Buddhappiya now follows Kaccāyana's order through the suffixes of descent, and this is Kaccāyana's second sutta as it is his sixth. His gloss adds `chaṭṭhiyantato` to Kaccāyana's `gottagaṇato` — the base is a clan name in ⑥ — and a remark that dispenses with any further explanation of the marker: `sabbattha ṇakārānubandho vuddhattho`, "everywhere the marker `ṇ` is for the purpose of strengthening"; the rest is as in 361–365, and the one thing to notice about `vacchāyano` is that there is no strengthening, the `a` of `vaccha` being followed by the conjunct `cch` ([364 (§400): Vuddhādisarassa vāsaṃyogantassa saṇe ca](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r364)). Where Kaccāyana's vutti runs through seventeen clan names in all three genders, Buddhappiya gives eight and calls the group an `ākatigaṇa`, a group defined by the form of its members rather than by an exhaustive list — any clan name of this shape belongs to it. The `vā` is the carried option of [361 (§344): Vā ṇa’pacce](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r361): `vacchāyano`, `vacchāno` or the phrase `vacchassa putto`, all three of which he sets side by side.

:::

> Vaccha kacca iccevamādito chaṭṭhiyantato gottagaṇato ṇāyana ṇāna iccete[^2] paccayā honti vā ‘‘tassāpacca’’miccetasmiṃ atthe. Sabbattha ṇa kārānubandho vuddhattho, sesaṃ pubbasamaṃ, saṃyogantattā vuddhiabhāvova viseso.

After `vaccha`, `kacca` and the like, the group of clan names (`gottagaṇa`), ending in the sixth form ⑥, the suffixes `ṇāyana` and `ṇāna` are optionally added in the sense "his offspring". Everywhere the marker `ṇ` is for the purpose of strengthening; the rest is as before; the only difference [here] is the absence of strengthening, because [the first vowel] is followed by a conjunct.

> Vacchāyano vacchāno vacchassa putto vā. Evaṃ kaccassa putto kaccāyano kaccāno, moggallassa putto moggallāyano moggallāno. Evaṃ aggivessāyano aggivessāno, kaṇhāyano kaṇhāno, sākaṭāyano sākaṭāno, muñcāyano muñcāno, kuñjāyano kuñjāno iccādi. Ākatigaṇo’yaṃ.

`vacchāyano`, `vacchāno`, or [the phrase] `vacchassa putto` ("Vaccha's son"). Likewise Kacca's son is `kaccāyano`, `kaccāno`; Moggalla's son `moggallāyano`, `moggallāno`; likewise `aggivessāyano`, `aggivessāno`; `kaṇhāyano`, `kaṇhāno`; `sākaṭāyano`, `sākaṭāno`; `muñcāyano`, `muñcāno`; `kuñjāyano`, `kuñjāno`, and so on. This is a group defined by form (`ākatigaṇa`).

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(vaccha + ṇāyana) | vacchāyano | a descendant of Vaccha — or `vacchassa putto` |
| 🚹①⨀(vaccha + ṇāna) | vacchāno | a descendant of Vaccha, with `ṇāna` |
| 🚹①⨀(kacca + ṇāyana) | kaccāyano | a descendant of Kacca — the name the grammar's author bears |
| 🚹①⨀(kacca + ṇāna) | kaccāno | a descendant of Kacca, with `ṇāna` |
| 🚹①⨀(moggalla + ṇāyana) | moggallāyano | a descendant of Moggalla |
| 🚹①⨀(moggalla + ṇāna) | moggallāno | a descendant of Moggalla — the elder's name |
| 🚹①⨀(aggivessa + ṇāyana) | aggivessāyano | a descendant of Aggivessa |
| 🚹①⨀(aggivessa + ṇāna) | aggivessāno | a descendant of Aggivessa |
| 🚹①⨀(kaṇha + ṇāyana) | kaṇhāyano | a descendant of Kaṇha |
| 🚹①⨀(kaṇha + ṇāna) | kaṇhāno | a descendant of Kaṇha |
| 🚹①⨀(sakaṭa + ṇāyana) | sākaṭāyano | a descendant of Sakaṭa — the one name of the list that takes the strengthening |
| 🚹①⨀(sakaṭa + ṇāna) | sākaṭāno | a descendant of Sakaṭa |
| 🚹①⨀(muñca + ṇāyana) | muñcāyano | a descendant of Muñca |
| 🚹①⨀(muñca + ṇāna) | muñcāno | a descendant of Muñca |
| 🚹①⨀(kuñja + ṇāyana) | kuñjāyano | a descendant of Kuñja |
| 🚹①⨀(kuñja + ṇāna) | kuñjāno | a descendant of Kuñja |

Without strengthening (`vaccha`) and with it (`sakaṭa`):

|     | vaccha + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇāyana       | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | vaccha + ~~sa~~ + ṇāyana        | §317 |
| →   | vaccha + (~~ṇ~~āyana)           | §396 |
| →   | vacch + (~~a~~ + āyana)         | §83  |
| →   | vacchāyana + (si→o)             | §104 |
| →   | **vacchāyano**                  |      |

|     | sakaṭa + sa (⑥⨀) + ṇāna         | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sakaṭa + ~~sa~~ + ṇāna          | §317 |
| →   | sakaṭa + (~~ṇ~~āna)             | §396 |
| →   | s + (a→ā) + kaṭa + āna          | §400, §405 |
| →   | sākaṭ + (~~a~~ + āna)           | §83  |
| →   | sākaṭāna + (si→o)               | §104 |
| →   | **sākaṭāno**                    |      |

:::

> ‘‘Kattikāya apacca’’nti viggahe –

In the analysis "Kattikā's offspring" —

[^2]: CST4 prints `iccate`; the form Buddhappiya uses everywhere else is `iccete`.
