The one-word rule for the great numbers, in Kaccāyana's place, read with the `nipātanā sijjhanti` of [423 (§391): Yadanupapannā nipātanā sijjhanti](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r423) carried into it: the numbers whose names no rule has stated are simply laid down by their own names. Buddhappiya's gloss adds their grammar — `koṭi` is feminine and singular, plural when groups are distinguished (`vaggabhede`), as `sataṃ` was at [415 (§393): Dasadasakaṃ sataṃ dasakānaṃ sataṃ sahassañca yomhi](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r415) — and the rule of the series, which Kaccāyana's vutti states for the first three steps only: each name is a hundred hundred-thousands of the one before (`satasahassānaṃ sataṃ koṭi`, "a hundred of hundred-thousands is a `koṭi`"), which his verse puts as "from place to place a hundred `lakkha` times", for the twenty numbers from `koṭi` to `asaṅkhyeyya`. His list agrees with Kaccāyana's in order and count (it has `nahutaṃ`, `ninnahutaṃ`, `akkhobhinī` where Kaccāyana's vutti has `nahutaṃ`, `ninnahutaṃ`, `akkhobhiṇī`, and `puṇḍarīkaṃ` before `padumaṃ` where Kaccāyana has the reverse). The `nahutaṃ` of this series is not the everyday ten thousand of [416 (§394): Yāva taduttari dasaguṇitañca](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r416). There is no option marker; like 416 the rule is a procedure, and the lead-in `‘‘Dve parimāṇāni etassā’’ti viggahe` turns to the last numeral suffix.

:::

> Yāsaṃ pana saṅkhyānaṃ aniddiṭṭhanāmadheyyānaṃ yāni rūpāni, tāni sakehi nāmehi nipaccante. Satasahassānaṃ sataṃ koṭi, itthiliṅgā, ekavacanantā ca, vaggabhede bahuvacanañca bhavati, koṭisatasahassānaṃ sataṃ pakoṭi, pakoṭisatasahassānaṃ sataṃ koṭippakoṭi. Evaṃ nahutaṃ, ninnahutaṃ, akkhobhinī, bindu, abbudaṃ, nirabbudaṃ, ahahaṃ, ababaṃ, aṭaṭaṃ, sogandhikaṃ, uppalaṃ, kumudaṃ, puṇḍarīkaṃ, padumaṃ, kathānaṃ, mahākathānaṃ, asaṅkhyeyyanti.

But the forms of those numbers whose names have not been stated [by any rule] are laid down by their own names. A hundred of hundred-thousands is a `koṭi` — feminine and singular, and plural when groups are distinguished; a hundred of hundred-thousand `koṭi` is a `pakoṭi`; a hundred of hundred-thousand `pakoṭi` is a `koṭippakoṭi`. In the same way `nahutaṃ`, `ninnahutaṃ`, `akkhobhinī`, `bindu`, `abbudaṃ`, `nirabbudaṃ`, `ahahaṃ`, `ababaṃ`, `aṭaṭaṃ`, `sogandhikaṃ`, `uppalaṃ`, `kumudaṃ`, `puṇḍarīkaṃ`, `padumaṃ`, `kathānaṃ`, `mahākathānaṃ`, `asaṅkhyeyyaṃ`.

:::tip[Examples]

Each name is 10⁷ times the one before it, from `koṭi` (10⁷) to `asaṅkhyeyya` (10¹⁴⁰).

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚺①⨀(koṭi) | koṭi | ten million, 10⁷ |
| 🚺①⨀(pakoṭi) | pakoṭi | 10¹⁴ |
| 🚺①⨀(koṭippakoṭi) | koṭippakoṭi | 10²¹ |
| 🚻①⨀(nahuta) | nahutaṃ | 10²⁸ |
| 🚻①⨀(ninnahuta) | ninnahutaṃ | 10³⁵ |
| 🚺①⨀(akkhobhinī) | akkhobhinī | 10⁴² |
| 🚻①⨀(bindu) | bindu | 10⁴⁹ |
| 🚻①⨀(abbuda) | abbudaṃ | 10⁵⁶ |
| 🚻①⨀(nirabbuda) | nirabbudaṃ | 10⁶³ |
| 🚻①⨀(ahaha) | ahahaṃ | 10⁷⁰ |
| 🚻①⨀(ababa) | ababaṃ | 10⁷⁷ |
| 🚻①⨀(aṭaṭa) | aṭaṭaṃ | 10⁸⁴ |
| 🚻①⨀(sogandhika) | sogandhikaṃ | 10⁹¹ |
| 🚻①⨀(uppala) | uppalaṃ | 10⁹⁸ |
| 🚻①⨀(kumuda) | kumudaṃ | 10¹⁰⁵ |
| 🚻①⨀(puṇḍarīka) | puṇḍarīkaṃ | 10¹¹² |
| 🚻①⨀(paduma) | padumaṃ | 10¹¹⁹ |
| 🚻①⨀(kathāna) | kathānaṃ | 10¹²⁶ |
| 🚻①⨀(mahākathāna) | mahākathānaṃ | 10¹³³ |
| 🚻①⨀(asaṅkhyeyya) | asaṅkhyeyyaṃ | the incalculable, 10¹⁴⁰ |

No derivation is offered: the names are laid down whole (`nipaccante`), and only their gender and number are stated. From `abbuda` to `paduma` the names are also those of the cold hells of the Kokālikasutta, whose lifespans stand in a like series.

:::

> Iccevaṃ ṭhānato ṭhānaṃ, satalakkhaguṇaṃ mataṃ; \
> Koṭippabhutinaṃ vīsa-saṅkhyānañca yathākkamaṃ.

Thus from place to place the twenty numbers beginning with `koṭi` \
are held to go, in order, each a hundred `lakkha` times [the last].

> ‘‘Dve parimāṇāni etassā’’ti viggahe –

In the analysis `dve parimāṇāni etassa`, "its measure is two" —

---

<a name="r418"></a>

### 418 (§392): Dvādito konekatthe ca (`ka` after `dvi` etc. in the sense of a group; and by `nipātana`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§392 (418): Dvādito ko’nekattheca" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m392" description="`ka` after `dvi` etc. in the sense of a group; and by `nipātana`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The last numeral rule in Buddhappiya's order — Kaccāyana states it before the hundreds, because his vutti needs `dvikaṃ` for `dvisataṃ`; Buddhappiya, who has derived `dvisataṃ` at [415 (§393): Dasadasakaṃ sataṃ dasakānaṃ sataṃ sahassañca yomhi](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r415) with the `ka` already dropped, can leave the group suffix to the end. His gloss adds `gaṇanato`, "after a numeral", the analytic phrase of the lead-in (`dve parimāṇāni etassa`, "its measure is two") and the sentence `dviko rāsi`, "a heap that is a pair", beside the neuter `dvikaṃ`; and he extends the list through `dasakaṃ` to `paṇṇāsakaṃ`, `satakaṃ`, `sahassakaṃ` — the last two the `satakaṃ`, `sahassakaṃ` of the canon's "a hundred-fold", "a thousand-fold". He passes over the `ca`, which Kaccāyana's vutti reads as drawing in `nipātanā sijjhanti` for the hundreds; that work is done at 415. There is no option marker. `anekattha` he leaves as the sutta has it: the sense of `aneka`, "not one", a group of that many.

:::

> Dvi iccevamādito gaṇanato ka ppaccayo hoti anekatthe. Dviko rāsi dvikaṃ. Evaṃ tikaṃ, catukkaṃ, pañcakaṃ, chakkaṃ, sattakaṃ, aṭṭhakaṃ, navakaṃ, dasakaṃ, paṇṇāsakaṃ, satakaṃ, sahassakaṃ iccādi.

After the numerals `dvi` and the rest the suffix `ka` is added in the sense of a group (`aneka`, "not one"). `dviko rāsi`, "a heap that is a pair"; `dvikaṃ`, "a pair". In the same way `tikaṃ`, `catukkaṃ`, `pañcakaṃ`, `chakkaṃ`, `sattakaṃ`, `aṭṭhakaṃ`, `navakaṃ`, `dasakaṃ`, `paṇṇāsakaṃ`, `satakaṃ`, `sahassakaṃ`, and so on.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🚹①⨀(dvi + ka) | dviko rāsi | a heap of two |
| 🚻①⨀(dvi + ka) | dvikaṃ | a pair |
| 🚻①⨀(ti + ka) | tikaṃ | a triad |
| 🚻①⨀(catu + ka) | catukkaṃ | a tetrad |
| 🚻①⨀(pañca + ka) | pañcakaṃ | a pentad |
| 🚻①⨀(cha + ka) | chakkaṃ | a hexad |
| 🚻①⨀(satta + ka) | sattakaṃ | a group of seven |
| 🚻①⨀(aṭṭha + ka) | aṭṭhakaṃ | a group of eight |
| 🚻①⨀(nava + ka) | navakaṃ | a group of nine |
| 🚻①⨀(dasa + ka) | dasakaṃ | a decad |
| 🚻①⨀(paṇṇāsa + ka) | paṇṇāsakaṃ | a group of fifty |
| 🚻①⨀(sata + ka) | satakaṃ | a group of a hundred |
| 🚻①⨀(sahassa + ka) | sahassakaṃ | a group of a thousand |

|     | dvi + yo (①⨂) + ka              | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | dvi + ~~yo~~ + ka               | §317 |
| →   | dvika + (si→aṃ)                 | §219 |
| →   | **dvikaṃ** \| dviko             |      |

|     | catu + yo (①⨂) + ka             | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | catu + ~~yo~~ + ka              | §317 |
| →   | catu + (k)ka                    | §28  |
| →   | catukka + (si→aṃ)               | §219 |
| →   | **catukkaṃ**                    |      |

`ka` begins with a consonant and leaves the numeral untouched; after `catu` and `cha` it is doubled by [§28 (40): Para dvebhāvo ṭhāne](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m28). The base is the plural of the analytic sentence (`dve`), its `yo` elided like any inflection.

:::

---

> _Saṅkhyātaddhitaṃ._

[Thus ends] the `saṅkhyātaddhita`, the numeral suffixes.

---

## Abyayataddhita (Adverbial suffixes)

> ‘‘Ekasmiṃ vāre bhuñjati, dvivāre bhuñjatī’’ti viggahe –

In the analysis `ekasmiṃ vāre bhuñjati`, "he eats on one occasion", `dvivāre bhuñjati`, "he eats on two occasions" —

---

<a name="r419"></a>

### 419 (§646): Ekādito sakissa kkhattuṃ (`kkhattuṃ` for `sakiṃ` after `eka` and the other numerals: "once", "twice") :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§646 (419): Ekādito sakissa kkhattuṃ" href="/kaccayana/8-unadikappa#m646" description="`kkhattuṃ` for `sakiṃ` after `eka` and the other numerals: 'once', 'twice'" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The seventh and last kind, the `abyayataddhita` — the derivatives that do not inflect, adverbs made with a suffix — opens with a rule Kaccāyana keeps for the miscellany at the end of his grammar, the Uṇādikappa (§646): a numeral with `kkhattuṃ` in place of `sakiṃ`, "once", is a `taddhita` and an indeclinable, so Buddhappiya brings it to stand with the three adverbial rules of the Taddhitakappa, [420 (§397): Vibhāge dhā ca](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r420) to [422 (§399): Kimimehi thaṃ](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r422), before the kaṇḍa closes with the general licence of [423 (§391): Yadanupapannā nipātanā sijjhanti](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r423). The lead-in gives the sense, `ekasmiṃ vāre bhuñjati`, "he eats on one occasion".

His gloss adds `gaṇanato`, "after a numeral", the sense `vāratthe`, "in the sense of occasions (`vāra`)", the elision of the derivative's `si` by [§221 (282): Sabbāsamāvuso pasagganipātādīhi ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m221) — the mark of an uninflected word, which every `abyayataddhita` will share, as he says at 422 — and a list running from `ekakkhattuṃ` to `sahassakkhattuṃ`, `bahukkhattuṃ` ("many times") and `katikkhattuṃ` ("how many times?"), which shows that "numeral" includes the indefinite `bahu` and the interrogative `kati`. There is no option marker. His closing remark, that "the mention of the numeral continues by a frog's leap" (`maṇḍūkagatiyā`), supplies the next rule with a word it lacks: `Vibhāge dhā ca` names no base, and the numeral is carried to it not from this rule's `ekādito` alone but from the `saṅkhyā` of [406 (§373): Saṅkhyāpūraṇe mo](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r406) and [408 (§374): Sa chassa vā](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r408), leaping over the rules between — the device Ruiz-Falqués notes at *Boundaries and Domains* §4.6 (`maṇḍūkapluti`).

:::

> Ekadviti iccevamādito gaṇanato sakissa ṭhāne vāratthe kkhattuṃ paccayo hoti. Ekakkhattuṃ, dvikkhattuṃ bhuñjati, ‘‘sabbāsamāvuso’’tiādinā si lopo. Evaṃ tikkhattuṃ, catukkhattuṃ, pañcakkhattuṃ, chakkhattuṃ, sattakkhattuṃ, aṭṭhakkhattuṃ, navakkhattuṃ, dasakkhattuṃ, satakkhattuṃ, sahassakkhattuṃ, bahukkhattuṃ, katikkhattuṃ.

After the numerals `eka`, `dvi`, `ti` and the rest, in place of `sakiṃ` ("once"), the suffix `kkhattuṃ` is added in the sense of occasions. `ekakkhattuṃ bhuñjati`, "he eats once"; `dvikkhattuṃ bhuñjati`, "he eats twice" — the `si` is elided by [§221 (282): Sabbāsamāvuso pasagganipātādīhi ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m221) and its companions. In the same way `tikkhattuṃ`, `catukkhattuṃ`, `pañcakkhattuṃ`, `chakkhattuṃ`, `sattakkhattuṃ`, `aṭṭhakkhattuṃ`, `navakkhattuṃ`, `dasakkhattuṃ`, `satakkhattuṃ`, `sahassakkhattuṃ`, `bahukkhattuṃ`, `katikkhattuṃ`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🔼(eka + kkhattuṃ) | ekakkhattuṃ bhuñjati | he eats once |
| 🔼(dvi + kkhattuṃ) | dvikkhattuṃ bhuñjati | he eats twice |
| 🔼(ti + kkhattuṃ) | tikkhattuṃ | three times |
| 🔼(catu + kkhattuṃ) | catukkhattuṃ | four times |
| 🔼(pañca + kkhattuṃ) | pañcakkhattuṃ | five times |
| 🔼(cha + kkhattuṃ) | chakkhattuṃ | six times |
| 🔼(satta + kkhattuṃ) | sattakkhattuṃ | seven times |
| 🔼(aṭṭha + kkhattuṃ) | aṭṭhakkhattuṃ | eight times |
| 🔼(nava + kkhattuṃ) | navakkhattuṃ | nine times |
| 🔼(dasa + kkhattuṃ) | dasakkhattuṃ | ten times |
| 🔼(sata + kkhattuṃ) | satakkhattuṃ | a hundred times |
| 🔼(sahassa + kkhattuṃ) | sahassakkhattuṃ | a thousand times |
| 🔼(bahu + kkhattuṃ) | bahukkhattuṃ | many times |
| 🔼(kati + kkhattuṃ) | katikkhattuṃ | how many times? |

|     | eka + smiṃ (⑦⨀) + kkhattuṃ      | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | eka + ~~smiṃ~~ + kkhattuṃ       | §317 |
| →   | ekakkhattuṃ + ~~si~~            | §221 |
| →   | **ekakkhattuṃ**                 |      |

|     | dvi + smiṃ (⑦⨀) + kkhattuṃ      | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | dvi + ~~smiṃ~~ + kkhattuṃ       | §317 |
| →   | dvikkhattuṃ + ~~si~~            | §221 |
| →   | **dvikkhattuṃ**                 |      |

The base is the numeral of the analytic phrase (`ekasmiṃ vāre`), whose inflection is elided; the suffix is cited with its doubled `kkh` and brings its own final `ṃ`, so that the derivative, treated as a noun and given `si`, loses it again as a particle.

> `sahassakkhattuṃ attānaṃ abhinimminitvā` \
> [(12S1 6.1.6: Brahmalokasutta)](https://tipitaka2500.github.io/tipitaka/12S1/6/6.1/6.1.6#1065) \
🔼(sahassa + kkhattuṃ) 🚹②⨀(atta) 🔽(abhinimminitvā) \
a thousand times | himself | having multiplied

:::

> ‘‘Ekena vibhāgenā’’ti viggahe –

In the analysis `ekena vibhāgena`, "by one division" —

> Maṇḍūkagatiyā saṅkhyā ggahaṇamanuvattate.

By a frog's leap the mention of the numeral continues [into the next rule].

---

<a name="r420"></a>

### 420 (§397): Vibhāge dhā ca (`dhā` in the sense of division; and `so`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§397 (420): Vibhāge dhā ca" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m397" description="`dhā` in the sense of division; and `so`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The first of Kaccāyana's three adverbial rules, in its order. Buddhappiya's gloss supplies the base — `ekādisaṅkhyāto`, "after the numerals from `eka`", the word brought by the frog's leap of [419 (§646): Ekādito sakissa kkhattuṃ](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r419) — and reads the `ca` more widely than Kaccāyana's vutti, which draws one suffix from it, `so`: by `ca` there are `jjha` after `eka` and `dvi` (`ekajjhaṃ`, `dvejjhaṃ`) and `so` after `sutta` and the like — after nouns, that is, not numerals (`suttaso`, `padaso`, `atthaso`, `yoniso`). For `dhā` he gives the alternative shapes of the stem: `dvidhā`, `dudhā`, `dvedhā`, `tidhā`, `tedhā`, the `du` being the `api` of [410 (§386): Tiye dutāpi ca](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r410) and the `e` of `dvedhā`, `tedhā` the `i` → `e` of [§404 (370): Tesu vuddhilopāgamavikāraviparītādesā ca](/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m404), which he cites. The rule has no option marker; the `vā` of `dudhā vā`, `tedhā vā` records the shapes a stem may take before the suffix, each by its own rule (the `kvaci` of §404), not a choice the rule opens. The derivatives are particles, and their `si` goes by [§221 (282): Sabbāsamāvuso pasagganipātādīhi ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m221), as the Bālāvatāra (¶266) says of `ekadhā`.

:::

> Vibhāgatthe ekādisaṅkhyāto dhā paccayo hoti. Ca saddena eka dvito jjha ca, suttā dito so ca. Ekadhā. Dvīhi vibhāgehi dvidhā, dudhā vā, dvedhā. Tīhi vibhāgehi tidhā, tedhā vā, ‘‘tesu vuddhī’’tiādinā ikārassekāro. Evaṃ catudhā, pañcadhā, chadhā, sattadhā, aṭṭhadhā, navadhā, dasadhā, satadhā, sahassadhā, katidhā, bahudhā.

In the sense of division (`vibhāga`), after the numerals `eka` and the rest the suffix `dhā` is added. By the word `ca`, `jjha` too after `eka` and `dvi`, and `so` after `sutta` and the like. `ekadhā` ("in one way"). `dvīhi vibhāgehi`, "by two divisions": `dvidhā`, or `dudhā`, `dvedhā` ("in two ways"). `tīhi vibhāgehi`, "by three divisions": `tidhā`, or `tedhā` — the `i` becoming `e` by [§404 (370): Tesu vuddhilopāgamavikāraviparītādesā ca](/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m404) and its companions. In the same way `catudhā`, `pañcadhā`, `chadhā`, `sattadhā`, `aṭṭhadhā`, `navadhā`, `dasadhā`, `satadhā`, `sahassadhā`, `katidhā`, `bahudhā`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🔼(eka + dhā) | ekadhā | in one way |
| 🔼(dvi + dhā) | dvidhā, dudhā, dvedhā | in two ways |
| 🔼(ti + dhā) | tidhā, tedhā | in three ways |
| 🔼(catu + dhā) | catudhā | in four ways |
| 🔼(pañca + dhā) | pañcadhā | in five ways |
| 🔼(cha + dhā) | chadhā | in six ways |
| 🔼(satta + dhā) | sattadhā | in seven ways |
| 🔼(aṭṭha + dhā) | aṭṭhadhā | in eight ways |
| 🔼(nava + dhā) | navadhā | in nine ways |
| 🔼(dasa + dhā) | dasadhā | in ten ways |
| 🔼(sata + dhā) | satadhā | in a hundred ways |
| 🔼(sahassa + dhā) | sahassadhā | in a thousand ways |
| 🔼(kati + dhā) | katidhā | in how many ways? |
| 🔼(bahu + dhā) | bahudhā | in many ways |

|     | eka + nā (③⨀) + dhā             | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | eka + ~~nā~~ + dhā              | §317 |
| →   | ekadhā + ~~si~~                 | §221 |
| →   | **ekadhā**                      |      |

|     | dvi + hi (③⨂) + dhā             | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | dvi + ~~hi~~ + dhā              | §317 |
| →   | dvidhā + ~~si~~                 | §221 |
| →   | **dvidhā** \| dudhā \| dvedhā   |      |

|     | ti + hi (③⨂) + dhā              | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ti + ~~hi~~ + dhā               | §317 |
| →   | t(i→e)dhā                       | §404 |
| →   | tedhā + ~~si~~                  | §221 |
| →   | **tedhā** \| tidhā              |      |

The base is the numeral of the analytic phrase in the third form (`ekena`, `dvīhi`); `dhā` begins with a consonant and leaves it untouched, except where §404 turns `dvi`, `ti` into `dve`, `te`, and `dudhā` has the `du` of [410 (§386): Tiye dutāpi ca](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r410).

:::

> Jjhappaccaye ekadhā karotīti ekajjhaṃ. Evaṃ dvejjhaṃ.

With the suffix `jjha`: `ekadhā karoti`, "he makes into one", `ekajjhaṃ` ("together"). In the same way `dvejjhaṃ` ("in two", "divided").

> So paccaye suttena vibhāgena suttaso. Evaṃ byañjanaso, padaso, atthaso, bahuso, sabbākārena sabbaso, upāyaso, hetuso, ṭhānaso, yoniso.

With the suffix `so`: `suttena vibhāgena`, "by division into suttas", `suttaso` ("sutta by sutta"). In the same way `byañjanaso`, `padaso`, `atthaso`, `bahuso`; `sabbākārena`, "in every way", `sabbaso`; `upāyaso`, `hetuso`, `ṭhānaso`, `yoniso`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🔼(eka + jjha) | ekajjhaṃ | together, in one |
| 🔼(dvi + jjha) | dvejjhaṃ | in two, divided |
| 🔼(sutta + so) | suttaso | sutta by sutta |
| 🔼(byañjana + so) | byañjanaso | syllable by syllable |
| 🔼(pada + so) | padaso | word by word |
| 🔼(attha + so) | atthaso | according to the sense |
| 🔼(bahu + so) | bahuso | in many ways |
| 🔼(sabba + so) | sabbaso | in every way, wholly |
| 🔼(upāya + so) | upāyaso | by way of means |
| 🔼(hetu + so) | hetuso | by way of cause |
| 🔼(ṭhāna + so) | ṭhānaso | by way of occasion |
| 🔼(yoni + so) | yoniso | by way of origin — thoroughly, wisely |

|     | eka + jjha                      | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ekajjha + (si→aṃ)               | §219 |
| →   | **ekajjhaṃ**                    |      |

|     | sutta + nā (③⨀) + so            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | sutta + ~~nā~~ + so             | §317 |
| →   | suttaso + ~~si~~                | §221 |
| →   | **suttaso**                     |      |

`ekajjhaṃ` and `dvejjhaṃ` keep a neuter `aṃ`, as adverbs in the second form do; `dvejjhaṃ` has the `dve` of §404. The `so` derivatives are the canon's `padaso dhammaṃ vāceti`, `suttaso anubyañjanaso`, `yoniso manasikāro`.

:::

---

<a name="r421"></a>

### 421 (§398): Sabbanāmehi pakāravacane tu thā (`thā` after pronouns in the sense of manner; and `thattā`) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§398 (421): Sabbanāmehi pakāravacane tu thā" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m398" description="`thā` after pronouns in the sense of manner; and `thattā`" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The second adverbial rule, in Kaccāyana's order. Buddhappiya's gloss reads `tu` as Kaccāyana's vutti does, as adding the suffix `thattā`, and adds a definition of `pakāra`: "the particular that distinguishes [one instance of] a general kind" — `sāmaññassa bhedako viseso` — so that `tathā`, "in that manner", picks out one way among the ways a thing can be. Where the vutti runs the paraphrase through all seven inflection forms, he gives three (`so pakāro`, `taṃ pakāraṃ`, `tena pakārena tathā`), adds the relative `yena pakārena yathā`, and extends the list with `ubhayathā`, "in both ways". `thattā` he reads with the Bālāvatāra (¶267) as the CST's Kaccāyana does not (it prints `thatthā`): `tathattā`, `yathattā`, `aññathattā`. There is no option marker; `tu` gathers in a second suffix, as at [387 (§360): Ṇyattatā bhāve tu](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r387). The derivatives are particles whose `si` is elided by [§221 (282): Sabbāsamāvuso pasagganipātādīhi ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m221).

:::

> Sabbanāmehi pakāravacanatthe thā paccayo hoti, tu saddena thattā paccayo ca. Sāmaññassa bhedako viseso pakāro, tassābhidhāneti attho, so pakāro tathā, taṃ pakāraṃ tathā, tena pakārena tathā, yena pakārena yathā. Evaṃ sabbathā, aññathā, itarathā, ubhayathā, thattā paccaye tena pakārena tathattā. Evaṃ yathattā, aññathattā.

After pronouns the suffix `thā` is added in the sense of the expression of manner (`pakāravacana`); and by the word `tu`, the suffix `thattā` too. A `pakāra` is the particular that distinguishes [an instance of] a general kind; the meaning is "in expressing that". `so pakāro`, "that manner", `tathā` ("thus"); `taṃ pakāraṃ`, "[in] that manner", `tathā`; `tena pakārena`, "by that manner", `tathā`; `yena pakārena`, "by which manner", `yathā` ("as"). In the same way `sabbathā`, `aññathā`, `itarathā`, `ubhayathā`. With the suffix `thattā`: `tena pakārena`, `tathattā`. In the same way `yathattā`, `aññathattā`.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🔼(ta + thā) | tathā | thus, in that manner |
| 🔼(ya + thā) | yathā | as, in which manner |
| 🔼(sabba + thā) | sabbathā | in every way |
| 🔼(añña + thā) | aññathā | otherwise |
| 🔼(itara + thā) | itarathā | otherwise |
| 🔼(ubhaya + thā) | ubhayathā | in both ways |
| 🔼(ta + thattā) | tathattā | in that manner |
| 🔼(ya + thattā) | yathattā | in which manner |
| 🔼(añña + thattā) | aññathattā | otherwise |

|     | ta + si (①⨀) + thā              | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ta + ~~si~~ + thā               | §317 |
| →   | tathā + ~~si~~                  | §221 |
| →   | **tathā**                       |      |

|     | ta + nā (③⨀) + thattā           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ta + ~~nā~~ + thattā            | §317 |
| →   | tathattā + ~~si~~               | §221 |
| →   | **tathattā**                    |      |

The one uninflected word answers to the pronoun in any inflection form, which is what the threefold paraphrase (`so`, `taṃ`, `tena pakārena`) shows; the pronoun's own inflection is elided and the stem is untouched by the consonant-initial suffix.

:::

> Ko pakāroti atthe –

In the sense `ko pakāro`, "what manner?" —

---

<a name="r422"></a>

### 422 (§399): Kimimehi thaṃ (`thaṃ` after `kiṃ` and `ima` in the sense of manner) :badge[apavada]{variant=success}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§399 (422): Kimimehi thaṃ" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m399" description="`thaṃ` after `kiṃ` and `ima` in the sense of manner" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

The third adverbial rule, in Kaccāyana's order, and the last operating rule of the kaṇḍa. Buddhappiya's gloss names the rules that shape the two stems: `kiṃ` becomes `ka` by the `ca` of [§227 (270): Kissa ka ve ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m227) (the Kaccāyana page reaches the same `ka` through [§229 (226): Sesesu ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m229)), and `ima` becomes `i` by [§234 (265): Imassi thaṃ dāni ha to dhesu ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m234), whose `thaṃ` is this suffix, with the doubling of the `th` after it. The paraphrases are cut to three forms each (`ko pakāro`, `kaṃ pakāraṃ`, `kena pakārena kathaṃ`; `ayaṃ`, `imaṃ`, `anena pakārena itthaṃ`). The article ends with a note on the whole section: the words ending in the suffixes from `kkhattuṃ` to `thaṃ` are `abyayataddhita`, so that after they have been treated as nouns and given an inflection, the inflection is elided by [§221 (282): Sabbāsamāvuso pasagganipātādīhi ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m221); and the words ending in `to` and the other suffixes that stand for an inflection — the `to`, `tra`, `tha`, `dhi`, `hiṃ`, `dā`, `dāni`, `rahi` and the rest of [§248 (260): Kvaci to pañcamyatthe](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m248) and the rules after it, which the Nāmakaṇḍa treats under [260 (§248): Kvaci to pañcamyatthe](/rupasiddhi/2-namakanda#r260) — "are included here too, among the uninflected derivatives". This is Buddhappiya's own classification, binding his Nāmakaṇḍa's `vibhattippaccaya` section to this one. There is no option marker.

:::

> Kiṃima iccetehi thaṃ paccayo hoti pakāravacanatthe. ‘‘Kissa ka ve cā’’ti ettha ca saddena ki ssa kā deso. Kathaṃ, kaṃ pakāraṃ kathaṃ, kena pakārena kathaṃ, ayaṃ pakāro itthaṃ, imaṃ pakāraṃ itthaṃ. Anena pakārena itthaṃ, ‘‘imassi thaṃ dāniha to dhesu cā’’ti ima saddassa i kāro, dvittaṃ. Ettha hi kkhattuṃ ādithaṃ pariyosānappaccayantānaṃ abyayataddhitattā nāmabyapadesaṃ katvā vibhattimhi kate ‘‘sabbāsamāvuso’’tiādinā vibhattilopo, ‘‘kvaci to pañcamyatthe’’tiādinā vuttato ādippaccayantā ca idheva a byayataddhite saṅgayhanti.

After `kiṃ` and `ima` the suffix `thaṃ` is added in the sense of the expression of manner. By the word `ca` in [§227 (270): Kissa ka ve ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m227), `kiṃ` is replaced by `ka`: `kathaṃ` ("how?"); `kaṃ pakāraṃ`, "[in] what manner", `kathaṃ`; `kena pakārena`, "by what manner", `kathaṃ`. `ayaṃ pakāro`, "this manner", `itthaṃ` ("thus"); `imaṃ pakāraṃ`, `itthaṃ`; `anena pakārena`, "by this manner", `itthaṃ` — by [§234 (265): Imassi thaṃ dāni ha to dhesu ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m234) the word `ima` becomes `i`, and there is doubling. Here, for the words ending in the suffixes from `kkhattuṃ` to `thaṃ`, since they are `abyayataddhita`, once they have been treated as nouns and an inflection has been added, the inflection is elided by [§221 (282): Sabbāsamāvuso pasagganipātādīhi ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m221) and its companions; and the words ending in `to` and the other suffixes taught by [§248 (260): Kvaci to pañcamyatthe](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m248) and the rules that follow are included right here, among the uninflected derivatives.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🔼(kiṃ + thaṃ) | kathaṃ | how? in what manner? |
| 🔼(ima + thaṃ) | itthaṃ | thus, in this manner |

|     | kiṃ + si (①⨀) + thaṃ            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | kiṃ + ~~si~~ + thaṃ             | §317 |
| →   | (kiṃ→ka) + thaṃ                 | §227 |
| →   | kathaṃ + ~~si~~                 | §221 |
| →   | **kathaṃ**                      |      |

|     | ima + si (①⨀) + thaṃ            | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ima + ~~si~~ + thaṃ             | §317 |
| →   | (ima→i) + thaṃ                  | §234 |
| →   | i + (t)thaṃ                     | §28, §29 |
| →   | itthaṃ + ~~si~~                 | §221 |
| →   | **itthaṃ**                      |      |

The doubled `th` takes the unaspirated `t` first by [§29 (42): Vagge ghosāghosānaṃ tatiyapaṭhamā](/kaccayana/1-sandhikappa#m29); the suffix brings its own final `ṃ`, and the `si` the derivative takes as a noun is elided as in every word of the section.

:::

---

<a name="r423"></a>

### 423 (§391): Yadanupapannā nipātanā sijjhanti (forms not reached by a rule are established by direct statement) :badge[vidhyaṅga]{variant=tip}

:::tip[Kaccāyana]
<LinkCard title="§391 (423): Yadanupapannā nipātanā sijjhanti" href="/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m391" description="forms not reached by a rule are established by direct statement" />
:::

:::note[Analysis]

Kaccāyana states this rule about the rules among the numerals, before the group suffix `ka`; Buddhappiya moves it to the very end of the kaṇḍa, where it stands as the general licence for every form he has referred to it along the way — the `ka` of `hatthikā`, `samaṇako`, `puttako` at [384 (§359): Ālu tabbahule](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r384), the `sataṃ` and `sahassaṃ` of [415 (§393): Dasadasakaṃ sataṃ dasakānaṃ sataṃ sahassañca yomhi](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r415), the great numbers of [416 (§394): Yāva taduttari dasaguṇitañca](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r416) and [417 (§395): Sakanāmehi](/rupasiddhi/5-taddhitakanda#r417) — and as the bridge to the sections that follow, for which it will be invoked again. His gloss condenses Kaccāyana's long enumeration of domains to three heads: words not accounted for by any rule "from the side of the letters and the rest, from that of nouns, prefixes and particles, or from that of compounds, `taddhita` and the rest" are established by direct statement (`nipātana`). "From the `taddhita` side, first" (`taddhitato tāva`), he lays down in a verse the time adverbs `ajja` ("today"), `sajju` ("at once", "the same day") and `aparajju` ("the next day"), with `a` for `ima` and `sa` for `samāna`, and then the suffix `tana` after such particles in the sense "belonging to [that time]": `ajjatanaṃ`, `svātanaṃ`, `purātanaṃ`, `hiyyattanaṃ`, with the feminines `ajjatanī` and `hiyyattanī` — the very names of two tenses of the Ākhyātakaṇḍa. None of these forms is derived by a rule; the verse's `siyā`, "let there be", states them. The rule is a `vidhyaṅga`, a procedural rule, and carries no option marker; `nipātana` is the grammar's way of admitting what usage has and the rules do not reach.

:::

> Ye saddā lakkhaṇena anupapannā a niddiṭṭhalakkhaṇā akkharādito, nāmopasagganipātato vā samāsataddhitādito vā, te nipātanā sijjhanti.

Those words which are not accounted for by a rule — for which no rule has been stated — whether from the side of the letters and the rest, from that of nouns, prefixes and particles, or from that of compounds, `taddhita` and the rest, are established by direct statement (`nipātana`).

> Taddhitato tāva –

From the `taddhita` side, first:

> Imasmā jja siyā kāle, samānāparato jju ca; \
> Imasaddassa’kāro ca, samānassa ca so siyā.

After `ima`, in the sense of time, let there be `jja`, and `jju` after `samāna` and `apara`; \
and let `a` stand for the word `ima`, and `sa` for `samāna`.

> Imasmiṃ kāle, imasmiṃ divase vā ajja, samāne kāle sajju, aparasmiṃdivase aparajju. Nipātehi bhavatthe tana ppaccayo. Ajja bhavaṃ ajjatanaṃ, ajja bhavā ajjatanī, sve bhavaṃ svātanaṃ. Evaṃ purātanaṃ, hiyyo bhavaṃ hiyyattanaṃ, hiyyo bhavā hiyyattanī iccādi.

`imasmiṃ kāle`, "at this time", or `imasmiṃ divase`, "on this day": `ajja` ("today"). `samāne kāle`, "at the same time": `sajju` ("at once"). `aparasmiṃ divase`, "on the following day": `aparajju`. After particles, in the sense "being [at that time]", the suffix `tana`. `ajja bhavaṃ`, "being today", `ajjatanaṃ` ("of today"); `ajja bhavā`, `ajjatanī`; `sve bhavaṃ`, "being tomorrow", `svātanaṃ`. In the same way `purātanaṃ` ("of old"); `hiyyo bhavaṃ`, "being yesterday", `hiyyattanaṃ`; `hiyyo bhavā`, `hiyyattanī`; and so on.

:::tip[Examples]

| lemma | inflected | meaning |
| ----- | --------- | ------- |
| 🔼(ima + jja) | ajja | today |
| 🔼(samāna + jju) | sajju | at once, the same day |
| 🔼(apara + jju) | aparajju | the next day |
| 🚻①⨀(ajja + tana) | ajjatanaṃ | of today |
| 🚺①⨀(ajja + tana + ī) | ajjatanī | of today — the aorist tense |
| 🚻①⨀(sve + tana) | svātanaṃ | of tomorrow |
| 🚻①⨀(purā + tana) | purātanaṃ | of old |
| 🚻①⨀(hiyyo + tana) | hiyyattanaṃ | of yesterday |
| 🚺①⨀(hiyyo + tana + ī) | hiyyattanī | of yesterday — the imperfect tense |

|     | ima + smiṃ (⑦⨀) + jja           | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ima + ~~smiṃ~~ + jja            | §317 |
| →   | (ima→a) + jja                   | §391 |
| →   | ajja + ~~si~~                   | §221 |
| →   | **ajja**                        |      |

|     | samāna + smiṃ (⑦⨀) + jju        | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | samāna + ~~smiṃ~~ + jju         | §317 |
| →   | (samāna→sa) + jju               | §391 |
| →   | sajju + ~~si~~                  | §221 |
| →   | **sajju**                       |      |

|     | ajja + tana                     | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ajjatana + (si→aṃ)              | §219 |
| →   | **ajjatanaṃ**                   |      |

|     | ajjatana + ī (🚺)               | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | ajjatan + (~~a~~ + ī)           | §238, §83 |
| →   | ajjatanī + ~~si~~               | §220 |
| →   | **ajjatanī**                    |      |

|     | hiyyo + tana                    | rule |
| --- | ------------------------------- | ---- |
| →   | hiyy(o→a) + (t)tana             | §404, §28 |
| →   | hiyyattana + (si→aṃ)            | §219 |
| →   | **hiyyattanaṃ**                 |      |

`ajja`, `sajju` and `aparajju` are particles and lose their `si` by [§221 (282): Sabbāsamāvuso pasagganipātādīhi ca](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m221); the `tana` derivatives are adjectives, with the feminine `ī` of [§238 (187): Nadādito vā ī](/kaccayana/2-namakappa#m238). In `svātanaṃ` the `e` of `sve` becomes `ā`, in `hiyyattanaṃ` the `o` of `hiyyo` becomes `a` and the `t` is doubled — changes of the kind [§404 (370): Tesu vuddhilopāgamavikāraviparītādesā ca](/kaccayana/5-taddhitakappa#m404) admits. `ajjatanī` and `hiyyattanī`, "[the tense] of today" and "[the tense] of yesterday", are the names of the aorist and the imperfect in the Ākhyātakaṇḍa.

:::

---

> _Abyayataddhitaṃ._

[Thus ends] the `abyayataddhita`, the uninflected derivatives.

---

> Sāmaññavuttibhāvatthā-byayato taddhitaṃ tidhā; \
> Tatrādi catudhāpaccā-nekatthassatthisaṅkhyāto.

The `taddhita` is threefold: of general application, in the abstract sense, and uninflected; \
the first of these is fourfold: of descent, of many senses, of possession and of number.

The closing verse counts the kaṇḍa's sections as three kinds and four sub-kinds: the `sāmaññavutti` suffixes, whose derivatives are ordinary nouns and adjectives of three genders — descent (361–372), the many senses (373–386), possession (398–405) and number (406–418) — then the abstract suffixes (387–389) and the uninflected derivatives (419–423). The suffixes of comparison (390–397) are not named separately.

---

> _Iti padarūpasiddhiyaṃ taddhitakaṇḍo pañcamo._

Thus ends the fifth section of the Padarūpasiddhi, the Taddhitakaṇḍa, on secondary derivation.

---
